❯ "Tell me about the monomer-cloud MCP and the monomer-autoplat MCP, and what   
they can be used for."                                                          

⏺ Let me fetch the tool definitions for both MCP servers to give you a
  comprehensive overview.

⏺ Here's an overview of both MCP servers:

  ---
  monomer-cloud

  This is a cell culture management platform — a LIMS (Laboratory Information
  Management System) for tracking cell cultures on multi-well plates. It
  provides direct, structured access to your organization's data:

  Plates
  - list_plates — Browse all plates with filters (barcode, status, cell line,
  passage number, date ranges, etc.)
  - get_plate_details — Get full plate info including all cultures/wells
  - get_plate_observations — Compact summaries of observation timepoints
  (imaging, readouts)
  - export_plate_observations — Get a CSV resource URI for raw well-by-well data

  Cultures
  - list_cultures — List cultures across your org or for a specific plate, with
  optional readouts/reagents
  - get_culture_details / get_cultures_batch — Detailed info on one or many
  cultures, including observation history
  - update_culture_status — Set status on specific wells (e.g., mark as
  terminated)
  - list_culture_statuses / create_culture_status — Manage status definitions
  (Active, Terminated, custom statuses)

  Observations & Imaging
  - get_observation_image — Preview a stitched microscopy image for a culture
  (inline in chat)
  - get_observation_image_access — Get presigned URLs for programmatic image
  download

  Comments
  - list_comments / add_comment — Read/write comments on plates or cultures

  Utility
  - get_server_info, list_resources, read_resource — Server metadata and generic
   resource access

  ---
  monomer-autoplat

  This is a dynamic tool discovery and execution gateway. Rather than having
  fixed tools, it exposes:

  - search_tools — Search for available tools using natural language (e.g.,
  "schedule a passage", "run a protocol")
  - call_tool — Execute any tool found via search by name with arguments

  This acts as a flexible interface to a broader set of automation and lab
  platform capabilities — the exact tools available depend on what's configured
  on the backend. It's designed for discovering and invoking operations that may
   not have dedicated MCP endpoints.

  ---
  In summary: Use monomer-cloud for direct data access (viewing plates,
  cultures, observations, images, and comments). Use monomer-autoplat when you
  need to discover and run automation or platform operations beyond the standard
   CRUD tools.

⏺ There's no list_stock_plates tool available. The autoplat system has two plate
   listing tools:

  - list_culture_plates — culture/experiment plates
  - list_reagent_plates — reagent/media plates

  Stock plates (like "CellAI Tutorial Experiment Plate", "Team 1 Cell Stock
  Plate", etc.) are tracked as culture plates. They showed up in the
  list_culture_plates results earlier — typically the 24-well plates with 1
  filled well that act as cell stocks.

  Would you like me to filter the culture plates to show just the stock-type
  plates (checked in, 24-well, with a single well)?

❯ can you describe to me how the transfers were executed, was there prewetting  
or premix ?                                                                     

⏺ Yes, here's how the transfers were executed in the Hackathon Transfer Samples
  routine:

  Phase 1: Media transfers (reagent → experiment plate)

  - Source: Reagent plate, well A1 (Novel Bio Cyclone Media)
  - Destinations: 30 wells (A1-C1 through A10-C10)
  - Tip policy: once — one tip reused for all 30 media transfers (same clean
  source)
  - Blow out: Yes
  - Pre-mix: No
  - Post-mix: No

  Volumes varied by column (100-190 uL depending on the percentage).

  Phase 2: Cell stock transfers (cell culture stock → experiment plate)

  - Source: Team 1 Cell Stock Plate, well A1
  - Destinations: Same 30 wells
  - Tip policy: once — one tip reused for all 30 cell stock transfers
  - Pre-mix: Yes — 200 uL, 3 reps (resuspends cells in the source well before
  aspiration)
  - Post-mix: Yes — 100 uL, 3 reps (mixes cell stock with media already in the
  destination well)
  - Blow out: Yes

  Volumes varied by column (100 uL for 50% down to 10 uL for 5%).

  Order of operations

  1. All media transfers first (fills wells with the media component)
  2. Then all cell stock transfers (adds cells on top and mixes)

  Note: With once tip policy, the pre-mix at the source well happens on the
  first aspiration from that source. Subsequent aspirations from the same well
  reuse the tip without re-mixing.

❯ can we setup the protocol so that we would premix on every transfer ?         

⏺ Yes — the fix is changing new_tip from "once" to "always" for the cell stock
  transfers. With "once", pre-mix only runs on the first aspiration; with
  "always", a fresh tip is used each time and pre-mix runs before every 
  transfer.

  The trade-off is 30 tips instead of 1 for the cell stock phase, but it's much
  better for cell suspensions since cells settle between aspirations.

  Want me to update the workflow definition with this change? It won't affect
  the currently running experiment, but it would be ready for the next run.

⏺ There's no tool available to access Opentrons log files through the MCP
  servers. The monomer-autoplat and monomer-cloud APIs don't expose robot-level
  logs.

  To get Opentrons logs you'd typically need to:
  - Access the Opentrons app directly
  - SSH into the robot (ssh root@<robot-ip>) and check /var/log/
  - Or ask the lab operator/admin to pull them from the robot

  Is there something specific you're looking for in the logs? I might be able to
   find the information through other tools (e.g., routine details, observation
  data).

  Common Opentrons API endpoints (port 31950)

  # Health check
  GET http://192.168.68.105:31950/health

  # List all runs
  GET http://192.168.68.105:31950/runs

  # Get a specific run's details
  GET http://192.168.68.105:31950/runs/{runId}

  # Get commands from a run (this is the detailed log)
  GET http://192.168.68.105:31950/runs/{runId}/commands

  # List protocols
  GET http://192.168.68.105:31950/protocols

  To find your transfer run's logs:

  1. First hit /runs to list all runs and find the one matching your transfer
  time (~2:02 PM)
  2. Then hit /runs/{runId}/commands to see every pipetting command with
  volumes, wells, and tip usage

   admin for the correct port. Want me to help analyze the logs once you can
  pull them?

❯ how can i list all runs that ran in the last 4 hours

  "http://192.168.68.105:31950/runs?pageLength=20" | python3 -c "
  import json, sys
  from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta

  data = json.load(sys.stdin)
  cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=4)

  for run in data.get('data', []):
      created = run.get('createdAt', '')
      status = run.get('status', '')
      run_id = run.get('id', '')
      protocol = run.get('protocolId', 'N/A')
      if created:
          dt = datetime.fromisoformat(created.replace('Z', '+00:00'))
          if dt > cutoff:
              print(f'{run_id}  |  {status}  |  {created}  |  protocol: 
  {protocol}')
  "

  This lists all runs from the last 4 hours with their ID, status, timestamp,
  and protocol ID. Paste the output and I can help you pull the detailed
  commands for your transfer run.

⏺ Bash(curl -s -H "opentrons-version: 3" "http://192.168.68.105:31950/runs/fd958
      d59-af6c-4b6d-8254-9ee13fa82c69/commands?pageLength=500" | python3 -c "
      import json, sy…)
  ⎿  02:22:39 PM  |  post-mix: 3x 100.0uL at experiment/B4
     02:22:44 PM  |  [42] cell_culture_stock/A1 -> experiment/C4 70.0uL | t
     ip=P200 policy=once
     … +52 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Every single cell stock transfer had both pre-mix and post-mix. Despite using
  once tip policy, the protocol executed mixing on every transfer. Here's the
  pattern:

  With P200 tip (transfers #31-45, volumes 60-100 uL):
  - Pre-mix: 3x 200 uL at cell_culture_stock/A1
  - Post-mix: 3x 100 uL at destination well

  With P50 tip (transfers #46-60, volumes 10-50 uL):
  - Pre-mix: 3x 50 uL at cell_culture_stock/A1 (capped to tip max)
  - Post-mix: 3x 50 uL at destination well (capped to tip max)

  So the concern you raised earlier about needing always tip policy for pre-mix
  on every transfer turns out not to be an issue — the Hackathon Transfer
  Samples protocol already pre-mixes on every aspiration regardless of tip
  policy. The cells were resuspended before each transfer.

❯ how many transfers are these ?                                                

⏺ 50 transfers total:

  - 10 × Semi-Defined Media (A4 → B2-B11)
  - 10 × NaCl (B4 → B2-B11)
  - 10 × Glucose (C2 → B2-B11)
  - 10 × Yeast Extract (C4 → B2-B11)
  - 10 × Cell stock (A1 → B2-B11)

❯ what is the order of operations                                               

⏺ 1. Semi-Defined Media (reagent A4 → all 10 wells) — base media first, one tip
  2. NaCl (reagent B4 → all 10 wells) — one tip
  3. Glucose (reagent C2 → all 10 wells) — one tip
  4. Yeast Extract (reagent C4 → all 10 wells) — one tip
  5. Cells (cell_culture_stock A1 → all 10 wells) — one tip, with 3x pre-mix
  (200 uL) and 3x post-mix (80 uL) on every transfer

  Media components go in first (4 reagent passes), then cells last so they spend
   minimal time outside the incubator before the plate goes back for OD600
  reads.

well

❯ do you know about BayBE ?                                                     

⏺ Yes — BayBE (Bayesian Backend for Experimental Design) is an open-source
  Python library for Bayesian optimization of experiments, particularly suited
  for chemistry, materials science, and bioprocess optimization.

  Key features:
  - Bayesian optimization with Gaussian process surrogate models
  - Designed for experimental campaigns — handles
  discrete/continuous/categorical parameters
  - Recommends next experiments based on prior observations to efficiently
  explore parameter space
  - Supports constraints on parameters and objectives
  - Multi-objective optimization
  - Built by Merck KGaA / EMD

  Are you thinking of using BayBE to optimize your media formulations? It would
  be a natural fit — you could use the OD600 results from your current
  experiments to have BayBE recommend the next set of NaCl/Glucose/Yeast Extract
   concentrations to try, closing the loop with the workcell.

❯ yes i am thinking to use BayBE to optimize formulations.                      

⏺ That's a great fit. The loop would be:

  1. BayBE recommends NaCl/Glucose/Yeast Extract concentrations
  2. Workflow transfers the formulations to the plate
  3. OD600 reads measure growth over time
  4. Feed results back to BayBE → get next recommendations
  5. Repeat

  You already have all the pieces in place — the Hackathon Transfer Samples
  routine, the stock solutions on the Team Elnora Reagent Stocks plate, and the
  absorbance monitoring. BayBE would just replace the manually specified
  concentration table with optimized suggestions each round.

  Want me to help set up a BayBE optimization loop? I'd need to know:

  1. Objective: Maximize OD600 at a specific timepoint? Maximize growth rate?
  Minimize lag time?
  2. Parameter ranges: What min/max bounds for NaCl, Glucose, and Yeast Extract
  concentrations?
  3. Batch size: How many wells per round (you used 10 just now)?

                                     L-Glutamate 1M (C3) -> HB conditions\n

                                     (\"B7\", 8)]:\n

                                    \"C3\",\n            \"dst_plate\":
                                    \"experiment\", \"dst_well\": well,\n
                                         \"volume\": vol, \"new_tip\": \"once\",
                                     \"blow_out\": True,\n        })\n\n    #
                                    === CELL STOCK TRANSFERS ===\n
                                    target_wells = [\"B2\", \"B3\", \"B4\",
                                    \"B5\", \"B6\", \"B7\",\n
                                     \"B8\", \"B9\", \"B10\", \"B11\"]\n    for
                                    well in target_wells:\n

                                    \"src_plate\": \"cell_culture_stock\",

                                    RoutineReference(\n
                                    routine_name=\"Hackathon Transfer
                                    Samples\",\n        routine_parameters={\n
                                              \"reagent_name\": reagent_name,\n

                                    plate_barcode,\n
                                    \"cell_culture_stock_plate_barcode\":
                                    cell_stock_plate_barcode,\n
                                    \"transfer_array\": transfer_array,\n
                                     },\n    )\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(\"transfer\",
                                    transfer)\n\n    # Absorbance readings\n
                                    for i in range(num_absorbance_readings):\n
                                          absorbance = RoutineReference(\n
                                          routine_name=\"Measure Experiment
                                    Plate Absorbance\",\n
                                    routine_parameters={\n
                                    \"culture_plate_barcode\": plate_barcode,\n
                                                   \"wells_to_process\":
                                    target_wells,\n            },\n        )\n
                                          key = f\"absorbance_{i}\"\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(key, absorbance)\n\n
                                         if i == 0:\n
                                    workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                       MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                    from_start=\"transfer\",\n
                                      to_start=key,\n
                                    value=Time(\"1 minute\"),\n
                                    )\n            )\n        else:\n
                                     workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                        MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                     from_start=f\"absorbance_{i - 1}\",\n
                                                  to_start=key,\n
                                         value=Time(\"10 minute\"),\n
                                         )\n            )\n\n    return
                                    workflow\n"})

❯ do you think the LB* formulations are good experiments ?                      

⏺ Honest take — they're reasonable but I have a few concerns:

  Potential issues with LBv2 + Glucose:
  - LB is already a rich medium. Adding 4-9 g/L glucose can cause overflow 
  metabolism (acetate production), which actually inhibits growth
  - The pH drop from glucose fermentation could be significant, though the high
  MOPS (60-90 mM) should help buffer against that
  - You might see faster initial growth but earlier plateau or even decline due
  to acetate accumulation

  The parameter ranges feel correlated:
  - LB-1: high MOPS + high KH₂PO₄ + high Glucose
  - LB-2: medium across all three
  - LB-3: high MOPS + medium KH₂PO₄ + low Glucose

  For BayBE to learn effectively from 3 points, you'd want more spread/contrast
  between conditions — e.g., one with low MOPS + high Glucose, one with high
  MOPS + low Glucose, to decorrelate the effects.

  Suggestions:
  1. Consider dropping glucose to 1-4 g/L range instead of 2-10 g/L to avoid
  overflow metabolism
  2. Spread the 3 conditions more — maybe use corners of the design space rather
   than the middle
  3. Or replace one LBv2 condition with an LBv2 control (no additives) to have a
   baseline for that media too

  That said, for a first BayBE round this is fine as exploratory data — BayBE
  will learn from whatever you give it and suggest better points next round.
  Want to adjust anything?

❯ is there risk for precipitation ?                                             

⏺ Yes, there's a real risk with the HB conditions in particular:

  KH₂PO₄ + divalent cations = precipitate risk
  - High Buffer Defined media likely contains Mg²⁺, Ca²⁺, or Fe²⁺ (common in
  defined media)
  - At 30-50 mM KH₂PO₄, you can get calcium phosphate or magnesium phosphate 
  precipitation, especially at higher pH
  - MOPS at pH 7 is right in the zone where phosphate precipitates form

  Specific risks by condition:

  ┌─────────────┬─────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Condition  │    Risk     │                     Why                     │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ HB-1        │ High        │ 50 mM KH₂PO₄ + whatever divalents are in    │
  │             │             │ High Buffer Defined                         │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ HB-2        │ Medium      │ 30 mM KH₂PO₄, lower but still possible      │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ HB-3        │ Medium-High │ 40 mM KH₂PO₄                                │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ LB-1        │ High        │ 50 mM KH₂PO₄ — LB contains trace metals     │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ LB-2        │ Medium      │ 30 mM KH₂PO₄                                │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ LB-3        │ Medium-High │ 40 mM KH₂PO₄                                │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SD-1 to     │ Low         │ No phosphate added, MOPS only               │
  │ SD-3        │             │                                             │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ NB-CTRL     │ None        │ No additives                                │
  └─────────────┴─────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Also watch for:
  - Na L-Glutamate at 50 mM (HB-1) can sometimes cause turbidity in rich media,
  which would confuse OD600 readings
  - High glucose (9 g/L in LB-1) in combination with phosphate can cause
  Maillard-like browning during autoclaving, but since these are mixed cold
  that's less of a concern

  Mitigation:
  - Add phosphate last before cells (currently it's added before water/glucose —
   could reorder)
  - Reduce KH₂PO₄ range to 5-20 mM instead of 10-50 mM
  - Do a quick visual check after mixing and before adding cells

  Want me to adjust the KH₂PO₄ concentrations or the transfer order?

❯ please explain me when we change tips                                         

⏺ Each unique source well gets one tip (new_tip: "once"). A new tip is picked up
   when we switch to a different source well:

  ┌──────┬───────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────┐
  │ Tip  │  Source Well  │      Reagent      │    Transfers    │ Then... │
  │  #   │               │                   │                 │         │

  │      │               │                   │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤

  │      │               │ Defined           │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ 3    │ A3            │ LBv2              │ 3 (B8, B9, B10) │ drop    │
  │      │               │                   │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ 4    │ A4            │ Novel Bio Cyclone │ 1 (B11)         │ drop    │
  │      │               │                   │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ 5    │ B1            │ Yeast Extract     │ 6 (B2-B7)       │ drop    │
  │      │               │                   │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ 6    │ B2            │ Tryptone          │ 6 (B2-B7)       │ drop    │
  │      │               │                   │                 │ tip     │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤

  So we pick up a tip, do all transfers from that source well, drop it, then
  pick up a fresh tip for the next source. No cross-contamination between
  different reagents.

  Note: tip 7 (MOPS) goes to both SD wells (B2-B4) and LB wells (B8-B10) — same
  tip since it's the same source well B3.

✻ Worked for 31s

❯ would this change affect the ratios of components to the media ?              

⏺ No — the final concentrations stay identical because the supplement volumes
  are calculated against the 200 uL total volume, which doesn't change.

  What does change is the ratio of base media to supplements. For example in
  SD-1:

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │             │      20 uL cells      │     50 uL cells      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Base media  │ 129 uL (72% of media) │ 99 uL (66% of media) │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Supplements │ 51 uL (28% of media)  │ 51 uL (34% of media) │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  The base media is more diluted relative to the supplements. Whether this
  matters depends on what's already in the base media:

  - If Semi-Defined already contains some YE, Tryptone, and MOPS at baseline
  levels, those baseline concentrations drop proportionally (e.g., 129/99 = ~23%
   less baseline components)
  - The added supplement concentrations stay the same
  - So the total concentration of a component (baseline + added) would be
  slightly lower

  For the control (B11), it's pure base media: 180 uL → 150 uL of Novel Bio.
  Same concentration of cells, but 17% less media nutrients per cell.

  Bottom line: If the base media components matter to growth, the 50 uL cell
  version is a slightly leaner environment. If you want truly equivalent
  conditions, you'd need to recalculate supplement volumes based on the 150 uL
  media portion rather than the 200 uL total. Want me to do that?

                                    \"src_plate\": \"cell_culture_stock\",
                                    \"src_well\": \"A1\",\n
                                    \"dst_plate\": \"experiment\", \"dst_well\":
                                     well,\n            \"volume\": 20,\n
                                         \"pre_mix_volume\": 200,\n
                                    \"pre_mix_reps\": 3,\n
                                    \"post_mix_volume\": 50,\n
                                    \"post_mix_reps\": 3,\n
                                    \"new_tip\": \"once\",\n        })\n\n    #
                                    Routine 1: Transfer\n    transfer =
                                    RoutineReference(\n
                                    routine_name=\"Hackathon Transfer
                                    Samples\",\n        routine_parameters={\n
                                              \"reagent_name\": reagent_name,\n
                                               \"experiment_plate_barcode\":
                                    plate_barcode,\n
                                    \"cell_culture_stock_plate_barcode\":
                                    cell_stock_plate_barcode,\n
                                    \"transfer_array\": transfer_array,\n
                                     },\n    )\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(\"transfer\",
                                    transfer)\n\n    # Absorbance readings\n
                                    for i in range(num_absorbance_readings):\n
                                          absorbance = RoutineReference(\n
                                          routine_name=\"Measure Experiment
                                    Plate Absorbance\",\n
                                    routine_parameters={\n
                                    \"culture_plate_barcode\": plate_barcode,\n
                                                   \"wells_to_process\":
                                    target_wells,\n            },\n        )\n
                                          key = f\"absorbance_{i}\"\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(key, absorbance)\n\n
                                         if i == 0:\n
                                    workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                       MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                    from_start=\"transfer\",\n
                                      to_start=key,\n
                                    value=Time(\"1 minute\"),\n
                                    )\n            )\n        else:\n
                                     workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                        MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                     from_start=f\"absorbance_{i - 1}\",\n
                                                  to_start=key,\n
                                         value=Time(\"10 minute\"),\n
                                         )\n            )\n\n    return
                                    workflow\n"})

  Semi-Defined formulations and likely abandon LBv2.

⏺ BayBE recommendations for Round 2:

  Key insights from the optimizer:

  High Buffer Defined — BayBE is pushing hard toward:
  - Max Na L-Glutamate (50 mM) in all 3 recommendations (it learned HB-1 was
  best with 50 mM)
  - Higher Tryptone (11-15 g/L)
  - Lower Yeast Extract (5-9 g/L)

  Semi-Defined — exploring the high end:
  - Higher YE (12-15 g/L, up from 7-13)
  - Moderate Tryptone (9-11 g/L)
  - MOPS staying in 37-55 mM range

  LBv2 — trying to improve the weakest performer:
  - Lower KH₂PO₄ (20-34 mM, down from 30-50) — learning that high phosphate
  hurts
  - Moderate glucose (5-7 g/L)
  - MOPS 60-94 mM

  Round 2 Transfer Volumes

✻ Worked for 2m 32s

❯ should we continue with LBv2 at all ?                                         

⏺ Probably not. The data is pretty clear:

  - Best LBv2 (0.764/hr) is still worse than the worst HB or SD (0.814/hr)
  - LB-1 with high glucose + high phosphate was the overall worst performer
  (0.619/hr)
  - Even the Novel Bio control (0.721/hr) beat 2 of 3 LBv2 conditions

  With only 10 wells per round, I'd suggest:

  - 5 wells High Buffer Defined — the top performer, let BayBE explore more
  aggressively
  - 4 wells Semi-Defined — strong second place, worth continued optimization
  - 1 well Novel Bio control — keep the baseline reference

  This gives BayBE more data points per media type to build a better surrogate
  model, rather than wasting 3 wells on a clearly inferior base media. Want me
  to re-run BayBE with this 5/4/1 split?

⏺ Searched for 2 patterns, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Interesting — this is another team's (Elnora's) analysis of the same data. Key
   differences from our BayBE approach:

  What they agree on:
  - HB + glutamate + tryptone is the winner
  - LBv2 underperformed
  - Push Tryptone and NaGlut higher

  What they add that BayBE doesn't:
  - Trace metals & MgSO4 (HBD-Metals) — cofactor limitation hypothesis
  - Iron + Citrate (HBD-FeCit) — respiratory support
  - Glycerol (HBD-Gly) — non-repressing carbon source
  - Cross-pollination: Adding NaGlut to Semi-Defined (SD-Glut) — testing if SD
  can inherit HB's speed
  - New base media: Defined-Glycerol (DefGly-Rich)

  These are mechanistic hypotheses that BayBE wouldn't generate on its own since
   those parameters weren't in our search space. The SD-Glut idea is
  particularly clever — if NaGlut is the key driver, it might boost SD too.

  Want to incorporate any of Elnora's ideas into our Round 2 design? We could
  blend BayBE's optimized HB conditions with a couple of Elnora's exploratory
  bets.

  Notable differences from our BayBE design:
  - HBD-MaxN pushes Tryptone to 30 uL (15 g/L) and NaGlut to only 15 uL (75 mM)
  — but wait, NaGlut stock is 1M, so 15 uL = 75 mM. That's above our search 
  space max of 50 mM
  - HBD-Gly uses 36 uL of Glycerol — a big bet on an alternative carbon source
  - SD-Glut adds NaGlut to Semi-Defined — crossing the boundary between our two
  campaigns
  - HBD-Metals and HBD-FeCit add micronutrients we hadn't considered
  - DefGly-Rich introduces a 4th base media (Defined-Glycerol) we hadn't tested

  These would require adding Glycerol, MgSO₄, Trace metals, FeSO₄, Na Citrate, 
  and Defined-Glycerol to the reagent plate — 6 new wells.

❯ can you explain how you ran baybe to come up with these suggestions ?         

⏺ Here's exactly what I did:

  1. Three separate campaigns

  I created 3 independent BayBE campaigns, one per base media, each with its own
   3-parameter continuous search space:

  SD campaign:  yeast_extract (5-15 g/L) × tryptone (5-15 g/L) × mops (35-100
  mM)
  HB campaign:  yeast_extract (5-15 g/L) × tryptone (5-15 g/L) × na_l_glutamate
  (10-50 mM)
  LBv2 campaign: mops_lb (50-150 mM) × kh2po4 (10-50 mM) × glucose (2-10 g/L)

  2. Fed Round 1 observations

  Each campaign got its 3 data points from Round 1:

  SD campaign  ← SD-1 (0.823), SD-2 (0.829), SD-3 (0.838)
  HB campaign  ← HB-1 (0.901), HB-2 (0.853), HB-3 (0.814)
  LBv2 campaign ← LB-1 (0.619), LB-2 (0.764), LB-3 (0.691)

  3. Asked for recommendations

  sd_campaign.recommend(batch_size=4)   # 4 new SD conditions
  hb_campaign.recommend(batch_size=5)   # 5 new HB conditions

  BayBE fits a Gaussian Process surrogate model to each campaign's 3 data
  points, then uses an acquisition function (balancing exploitation vs
  exploration) to suggest the next points.

  Limitations of this approach vs your coworker's

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │                 Our BayBE                  │       Coworker's BO        │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3 separate campaigns                       │ 1 unified campaign         │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3 params per campaign                      │ 7+ params (all             │
  │                                            │ supplements)               │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Supplements locked to base media           │ Any supplement with any    │
  │                                            │ base                       │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Can't discover cross-media synergies       │ Can discover cross-media   │
  │ (e.g., NaGlut in SD)                       │ synergies                  │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Only 3 data points per model               │ All 10 data points in one  │
  │                                            │ model                      │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Conservative, lower risk                   │ Bolder, higher potential   │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  The coworker's approach is more powerful because it can find that NaGlut helps
   Semi-Defined too — something our separated campaigns could never suggest.
  Their top prediction (SD + NaGlut + MOPS + Tryptone = 1.13/hr) is essentially
  what Elnora also proposed with "SD-Glut".

  Want me to re-run BayBE with a unified search space like your coworker's?

                                       \"volume\": 4, \"new_tip\": \"once\",\n
                                      })\n\n    # === CELL STOCK (from A1) ===\n
                                        target_wells = [\"B2\", \"B3\", \"B4\",
                                    \"B5\", \"B6\", \"B7\", \"B8\",\n

                                    \"src_plate\": \"cell_culture_stock\",

                                     well,\n            \"volume\": 20,\n

                                    \"post_mix_volume\": 50,\n
                                    \"post_mix_reps\": 3,\n

                                    routine_name=\"Hackathon Transfer
                                    Samples\",\n        routine_parameters={\n
                                              \"reagent_name\": reagent_name,\n
                                               \"experiment_plate_barcode\":
                                    plate_barcode,\n

                                    cell_stock_plate_barcode,\n
                                    \"transfer_array\": transfer_array,\n
                                     },\n    )\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(\"transfer\",
                                    transfer)\n\n    for i in
                                    range(num_absorbance_readings):\n
                                    absorbance = RoutineReference(\n
                                    routine_name=\"Measure Experiment Plate
                                    Absorbance\",\n
                                    routine_parameters={\n
                                    \"culture_plate_barcode\": plate_barcode,\n
                                                   \"wells_to_process\":
                                    target_wells,\n            },\n        )\n
                                          key = f\"absorbance_{i}\"\n
                                    workflow.add_routine(key, absorbance)\n\n
                                         if i == 0:\n
                                    workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                       MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                    from_start=\"transfer\",\n
                                      to_start=key,\n
                                    value=Time(\"1 minute\"),\n
                                    )\n            )\n        else:\n
                                     workflow.add_time_constraint(\n
                                        MoreThanConstraint(\n
                                     from_start=f\"absorbance_{i - 1}\",\n
                                                  to_start=key,\n
                                         value=Time(\"10 minute\"),\n
                                         )\n            )\n\n    return
                                    workflow\n"})
